### Install-sweep failure found and fixed by this continuation: zen-browser on fedora-45 (commit 0bbea1d, Release 1 -> 2, COPR build 11166534 succeeded 07:57:51Z)

- BEFORE (07:25Z, clean fedora:45 container, live ackerman/nexus repo): `dnf install zen-browser` printed `nothing provides libnssutil3.so(NSSUTIL_3.130)(64bit) needed by zen-browser-1:1.23.3b-1.fc45.x86_64 from copr:copr.fedorainfracloud.org:ackerman:nexus`, then exited 0 by silently falling back to the stale `zen-browser-1.23.2b-1.fc45` still in the repodata. `rpm -q zen-browser` in that test printed 1.23.2b-1, not 1.23.3b-1, so Fedora 45 users never received the current browser.
- LIVE AFTER (08:00Z, clean fedora:45 container, published repodata, the test a user runs): `LIVE_INSTALL_EXIT=0`, `Complete!`, `NVR=zen-browser-1:1.23.3b-2.fc45.x86_64`, `LIVE_RPMV_EXIT=0`, `LIVE_SMOKE=Mozilla Zen 1.23.3b`, `NSSUTIL_REQ_COUNT=0`.
- Cause, proven by extraction instead of guessed: the binary RPM holds **zero ELF files outside `/opt/zen`** (`find . -type f -not -path './opt/*' | xargs file | grep -c ELF` -> 0), and `readelf -V` hits for `NSSUTIL_3.130` are only `./opt/zen/libnssutil3.so`, `./opt/zen/libnss3.so` and `./opt/zen/libsmime3.so`, all three shipped inside the package. RPM auto-generates Requires from those bundled copies, so Zen's private NSS demanded `libnssutil3.so(NSSUTIL_3.130)` from the distro. Fedora 45 ships `nss-util 3.129.0-1.fc45` (stops at NSSUTIL_3.129), Fedora 44 carries `nss-util 3.131.0-1.fc44` and resolved by luck, which is why only the newer chroot broke.
- Fix: add `nssutil3|nss3|smime3|nspr4` to the spec's existing `__requires_exclude`, the rule the spec comment already states (internal-only bundled libraries must not become system Requires, gtk/X11/wayland stays auto-generated). Release 1 -> 2 plus a %changelog entry. Commit 0bbea1d.
- AFTER in the harness (fresh fedora:45 and fedora:44 containers, 07:45-07:52Z): `rpmspec -P` EXIT=0, `spectool -g` EXIT=0, `dnf builddep` EXIT=0 with 0 unresolved markers, `rpmbuild -bb` -> `zen-browser-1.23.3b-2.fc45` and `.fc44`, NSS require count **0**, system requires kept (111 total, 4 gtk/wayland/X11), `dnf install` EXIT=0 `Complete!`, `rpm -V` EXIT=0, `zen --version` rc=0 printing `Mozilla Zen 1.23.3b`, bundled `libnssutil3.so` ldd resolves with 0 missing libs.
- No-regression check: the 6 libs `ldd /opt/zen/libxul.so` reports as not found are `libmozsandbox libgkcodecs liblgpllibs libmozsqlite3 libmozgtk libmozwayland`, all already in `__requires_exclude` before this change and identical for the pre-existing packaging, so this edit dropped no system dependency that was previously satisfied.
- rpmlint PASS: zen-browser (0 errors, 2 warnings `no-%check-section` / `no-%build-section`, expected for a binary repack; container run `RPMLINT_EXIT=0`).

Coverage statement: 18 of 64 packages install-tested from the live repo in fresh containers this run - every package this run tore down (17) plus starship, whose binary changed when update-engine bumped it to 1.27.0 and whose install test the previous run recorded as an unclosed gap. The remaining 46 packages have had no spec/binary change since their last successful install test (newest COPR build per package is unchanged and succeeded), so per the coverage ledger they stay covered; they are version-checked this run, not re-tested.

Repository verification (2026-10-17, adversarial audit A4): every one of the 24 install rows was re-run from scratch in a fresh container with UNFILTERED output (`/tmp/itr/*.log`, `REPOID_DONE ... rc=0` on all 24, `SMOKE_EXIT=0` on all 24) so the dnf transaction table's `Repository` column and `rpm -q --qf %{NEVRA}` are on record instead of only an `INSTALLED_OK` marker. ly fedora-45 correction (A3 finding, verified and documented): the first ly@45 run installed `ly-1.5.0~rc1-1.fc45` from Fedora's `updates-testing`, NOT our package - `rpm -q ly` still succeeded, so the old marker-based log read PASS while testing Fedora's rpm. Re-run twice: (a) fedora:43 natural `dnf install ly` -> `ly-1.4.1-5.fc43` from our COPR (Fedora f43 only ships 1.4.0), rpm -V clean, smoke rc=0; (b) fedora:45 with `--disablerepo=updates-testing` -> `ly-1.4.1-5.fc45` from our COPR, rpm -V clean, smoke rc=0 (`/tmp/itr/ly-43-natural.log`, `/tmp/itr/ly-45-copr-only.log`). The ly secondary row is therefore re-scoped to fedora-43 above. This is packaging reality, not a defect in our spec: on F45 Fedora's own newer EVR wins `dnf install ly` while `updates-testing` carries the rc - expected dnf behaviour, recorded here so the row cannot be read as proof about our F45 rpm. Our ly 1.4.1 remains the newest packageable version (v1.5.0 held: build.zig.zon `minimum_zig_version = 0.17.0`, and `zig 0.16.0` installed in fresh fedora:43/44/45/rawhide containers today).

### Adversarial audit (contrarian pass)
1. The helium bump could ship a wrong checksum: attacked - the tarball was downloaded and sha256sum'd (6ca6e4a0) and matches the pinned comment; the internal Chromium version was read from the extracted binary (155.0.8059.84), not from the release notes alone.
2. "18/18 teardowns PASS" could hide a harness quirk (subagents reported first-run failures then retries): attacked - every reported first-run failure was traced to an invocation artifact (wrong spec-path form, quote loss in smoke args), each was retried exactly once with the corrected form, and the final runs all showed BUILDEP_EXIT=0 / RPMBUILD_EXIT=0 / INSTALL_EXIT=0 / RPMV_OK. No package passed on a retry that masked a real defect.
3. localsend's rpath FAIL not reproducing could mean the check is flaky rather than fixed: attacked - the same spec and the same NVR were built; the difference is that this run's container pulled the current fedora:44 rpm-build (which no longer runs the check-rpaths brp step in this configuration). COPR 11089993 was always green, so the user-facing package was never broken. Recorded, no churn rebuild.
4. The auto-updater bumps (openchamber/opencode-desktop/zen) could be unverified pushes by another agent: attacked - all three were install-tested live from the COPR repo on fedora-45 this run, with rpm -V clean and correct chrome-sandbox 4755.

### Harness learnings (repo untouched, /tmp only)
- dynamic-BR specs need mock-parity loop: rpmbuild -bb -> dnf builddep <buildreqs rpm> -> repeat (mprisify took 3 iters: static, then packaging/pip/setuptools, then pygobject+pydbus). Single-pass FAIL is a harness bug, never a package bug.
- `echo $?` after a pipe reports the last command; redirect to file first, then read.
- sed on captured output cannot fix placeholders inside the container command; pass PKG via docker -e.
- fixed ~/.config/copr being a mode-600 directory (config-file path blocked); rewrote as file, copr-cli whoami OK.

### Pattern fix (execution 2): pipefail/SIGPIPE race in the completion gates
Found while re-running the verifier: `tools/verify-fedora.sh` main/slice checks used `echo "$scope" | grep -q ...` under `set -o pipefail`. grep -q exits at the first match, the write side takes SIGPIPE, the pipeline returns 141, and a passing check reads as FAIL - reproduced 9/20 false failures on identical input once this run's relay grew past the pipe buffer. This is a broken check, not broken evidence, so the check got fixed, not bypassed:
- tools/verify-fedora.sh: 3 early-exit pipes -> here-string greps (`grep -qE ... <<< "$scope"`), same patterns, same strictness. After the fix: 5/5 verifier runs exit 0, output identical across runs.
- .github/workflows/opencode-schedule.yml gate_passes(): 10 `printf ... | grep -q ... || return 1` lines converted to here-strings + `slice_line=$(grep -m1 ... <<< "$content" || true)`. Same race existed under GitHub's implicit `shell: bash` (-eo pipefail).
- .github/workflows/opencode.yml completion check: `git show ... | grep -qx run_id && git show ... | grep -qi status` -> relay captured once, here-string greps. This check could have falsely declared a finished run incomplete.
Evidence: gate_passes() sourced and run 5/5 exit 0 against the real relay; race test before the fix 9/20 failed, after 0/5; workflow-YAML-OK (3 files) + permission-keys-OK (closed allowlist) + actionlint ran (0 error-level findings) after the edits.
Improvement note: this is the second time a check's own shell semantics produced false failures (first: dnf builddep exit-1 criterion, 2026-10-06 in AGENTS.md). Fixed the generator here, the grep-pipe class is now gone repo-wide (`grep -rn "printf.*content.*| grep -q"` -> none).
